  <div class="page-header">
    <h1>Public Data Access 
      <small><a href="/data/">Overview</a> / Data Specifications</small>
    </h1>
  </div>

  <div class="row">
    <div class="col-md-12">
      
      <p>This page contains a comprehensive description of the simulation snapshots, group catalogs, merger trees, and supplementary data sets. Differences and additions in TNG with respect to the original Illustris public data release are noted: aspects which have changed, or which are new in TNG, are <span class="newtng">marked in blue</span>, while those <span class="sametng">marked in green</span> are identical to Illustris.</p>

      <h3>1. Snapshots</h3>
      
      <a name="sec1a" class="anchor"></a>

      <h4>Organization</h4>
      
      <p>There are <span class="newtng">100</span> snapshots stored for every run. These include all particles/cells in the whole volume. 
      The complete snapshot listings, spacings and redshifts can be found in the API. Note that, unlike in Illustris, 
      <span class="newtng">TNG contains two different types of snapshots: 'full' and 'mini'</span>. While both encompass the entire volume, 
      'mini' snapshots only have a subset of particle fields available (detailed below). In TNG twenty snapshots are full, while the 
      remaining 80 are mini. The 20 full snapshots are:</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTableSm">
          <tr>
            <th> Snap </th><th> Scale factor </th><th> Redshift </th>
          </tr>
          <tr><td> 2  </td><td> 0.0769 </td><td> 12  </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 3  </td><td> 0.0833 </td><td> 11  </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 4  </td><td> 0.0909 </td><td> 10  </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 6  </td><td> 0.1    </td><td> 9   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 8  </td><td> 0.1111 </td><td> 8   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 11 </td><td> 0.125  </td><td> 7   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 13 </td><td> 0.1429 </td><td> 6   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 17 </td><td> 0.1667 </td><td> 5   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 21 </td><td> 0.2    </td><td> 4   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 25 </td><td> 0.25   </td><td> 3   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 33 </td><td> 0.3333 </td><td> 2   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 40 </td><td> 0.4    </td><td> 1.5 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 50 </td><td> 0.5    </td><td> 1   </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 59 </td><td> 0.5882 </td><td> 0.7 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 67 </td><td> 0.6667 </td><td> 0.5 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 72 </td><td> 0.7143 </td><td> 0.4 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 78 </td><td> 0.7692 </td><td> 0.3 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 84 </td><td> 0.8333 </td><td> 0.2 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 91 </td><td> 0.9091 </td><td> 0.1 </td></tr>
          <tr><td> 99 </td><td> 1      </td><td> 0   </td></tr>
        </table>
      </div>
      
      <p>Every snapshot is stored in a series of "chunks", i.e. more manageable, smaller-size files. The number of 
      chunks per snapshots is different for the different runs:</p>

      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTable">
          <tr>
          <th>Run</th>

          </tr><tr>
          <td> L205n2500TNG_DM </td><td> TNG300-1-Dark      </td><td> 15,625,000,000     </td><td> 75   </td><td> 930 GB        </td><td> 5.2 GB   </td><td> 57 TB </td>
          </tr>
        </table>
      </div>
      
      <p>Note that the snapshot data is <b>not</b> organized according to spatial position. Rather, <span class="sametng">particles within 
      the snapshot files are sorted according to their group/subgroup memberships</span>, according to the FoF or Subfind 
      algorithms. Within each particle type, the sort order is: GroupNumber, SubgroupNumber, BindingEnergy, where 
      particles belonging to the group but not to any of its subgroups ("fuzz") are included after the last subgroup.
      The following figure provides a schematic view of the particle organization within a snapshot, for <i>one 
      particle type</i>. Note that the truncation of a snapshot in chunks is arbitrary, thus halos may happen to be 
      stored across multiple, subsequent chunks. Similarly, the different particle types of a halo can be stored in 
      different sets of chunks.</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
      <img style="margin: 0 auto;" class="img-responsive" src="/static/data/specifications_snapshot.png">
      </div>
      
      <p class="caption"><b>Caption.</b> Schematic diagram of 
      the organization of particle/cell data within the snapshots for a single particle type. Within a type, particle 
      order is determined by a global sort of the following fields in this order: FoF group number, Subfind subhalo 
      number, binding energy, nearest FoF group number. This implies that FOF halos are contiguous, although they can 
      span file chunks. Subfind subhalos are only contiguous within a single group, being separated between groups by 
      an "inner fuzz" of all FOF particles not bound to any subhalo. Here $N_c$ indicates the number of file chunks, 
      $n_F$ the number of FOF groups, and $N_{S,j}$ the number of subhalos in $j^{\rm th}$ FoF group.</p>
      
      <a name="sec1b" class="anchor"></a>

      <h4>Contents</h4>
      
      <p>Every HDF5 snapshot contains several groups: "Header", "Parameters", "Configuration", and five "PartTypeX" groups, for the following particle 
      types (the DM only runs have a single PartType1 group):
      
      <ul>
        <li>PartType0 - GAS</li>
        <li>PartType1 - DM</li>
        <li>PartType2 - (unused)</li>
        <li>PartType3 - TRACERS</li>
        <li>PartType4 - STARS <b>&amp; WIND PARTICLES</b></li>
        <li>PartType5 - BLACK HOLES</li>
      </ul>
      
      <p>The most important fields of the <code>Header</code> group are given in the following table.</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTable">
          <tr><th colspan="4" class="ptHead">Header</th></tr>
          <tr>
          <th> Field </th>
          <th> Dimensions </th>
          <th> Units </th>
          <th> Description </th>
          </tr>
          <tr>
          <td> BoxSize
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Spatial extent of the periodic box (in comoving units).
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> MassTable
          </td><td> 6
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot/h $
          </td><td> Masses of particle types which have a constant mass (only DM).
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> NumPart_ThisFile
          </td><td> 6
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Number of particles (of each type) included in this (sub-)file.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> NumPart_Total
          </td><td> 6
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Total number of particles (of each type) across all (sub-)files of this snapshot, modulo $ 2^{32} $.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> NumPart_Total_HighWord
          </td><td> 6
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Total number of particles (of each type) across all (sub-)files of this snapshot, divided by $ 2^{32} $ and rounded downwards.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> Omega0
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> The cosmological density parameter for matter.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> OmegaLambda
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> The cosmological density parameter for the cosmological constant.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> Redshift
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> The redshift corresponding to the current snapshot.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> Time
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> The scale factor a (=1/(1+z)) corresponding to the current snapshot.
          </tr><tr>
          </td><td> NumFilesPerSnapshot
          </td><td> 1
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Number of file chunks per snapshot.
        </table>
      </div>

      <p>The <code>Parameters</code> and <code>Configuration</code> provide the complete set of parameter file options and run-time configuration 
      options used to run TNG. That is, they encode the fiducial "TNG Galaxy Formation Model". Many will clearly map to Table 1 of 
      <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.02970">Pillepich et al. (2018a)</a>, while others deal with more numerical/technical options. In the future, together with the release 
      of the TNG initial conditions and the TNG code base of AREPO, this will enable any of the TNG simulations to be reproduced.</p>
      
      <p>The complete snapshot field listings of the <code>PartTypeX</code> groups, including dimensions, units and descriptions, are given for all the 
      particles types in the following large table. Rows in <span class="newtng">blue</span> are new or different in some way with respect to original Illustris, while those in <span class="sametng">green</span> are unchanged.</p>

      <div class="table-responsive" style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTable tngsnaptbl">
        
          <tr><th colspan="7" class="ptHead"> PartType0 (gas) </th></tr> <a name="parttype0" class="anchor"></a>
          <tr>
          <th> Field </th>
          <th style="width:5%;"> Full Snaps </th>
          <th style="width:5%;"> Mini Snaps </th>
          <th style="width:5%;"> Subbox Snaps </th>
          <th style="width:3%;"> Dims </th>
          <th style="width:15%;"> Units </th>
          <th> Description </th>
          </tr>
          <tr><td class="newtng"> CenterOfMass
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N,3
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Spatial position of the center of mass, which in general differs from the geometrical center of the Voronoi cell (the offset should be small). Comoving coordinate.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Coordinates
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N,3
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Spatial position within the <b>periodic</b> simulation domain of BoxSize. Comoving coordinate.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Density
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ (10^{10} M_\odot/h) / (ckpc/h)^3 $
          </td><td> Comoving mass density of cell (calculated as mass/volume).
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> ElectronAbundance
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Fractional electron number density with respect to the total hydrogen number density, so $n_e = \rm{ElectronAbundance} * n_H$ where $n_H = X_H * \rho / m_p$. Use with caution for star-forming gas (see comment below for NeutralHydrogenAbundance).
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="newtng"> EnergyDissipation
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> - (^)
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ (1/a) 10^{10} M_{\odot}/\rm{kpc} (\rm{km/s})^3 $
          </td><td> Shock finder output: the dissipated energy rate (amount of kinetic energy irreversibly transformed into thermal energy). Note units correspond to (Energy/Time). Multiply by $1/a$ to obtain physical units. 
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GFM_AGNRadiation
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ erg / s / cm^{2} * (4\pi) $
          </td><td> Bolometric intensity (physical units) at the position of this cell arising from the radiation fields of nearby AGN. One should divide by $4\pi$ to obtain the flux at this location, in the sense of $F = L / (4 \pi R^2)$.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GFM_CoolingRate

          <tr><td class="newtng"> FluidQuantities
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span> (*)
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span> (*)
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Of the various quantities recorded for tracers in Illustris, we now have kept only one, similar to "Last_Star_Time". Therefore, this dataset is now a single number for each tracer, and contains information on the previous presence of the tracer inside a particle of type 4 (either a real star, or wind, particle). If the tracer has never been in a particle of type 4, the value is 0. (i) For tracers that are currently in gas cells or black holes, the range of possible values is [-All.TimeMax, All.TimeMax], as follows: if the tracer was last seen in a 'real' star particle, the value is simply the time it came back to the gas phase, i.e. the possible range of values is [0, All.TimeMax]. If the tracer was last seen in a wind particle, the value is the time it came back to the gas phase, multiplied by (-1), i.e. the possible range of values is [-All.TimeMax, 0]. (ii) For tracers that are currently in a 'real' star particle, the value is fixed at 2*All.TimeMax. (iii) For tracers that are currently in a wind particle, the value equals whatever value they had before joining the wind plus 3*All.TimeMax, i.e. the possible range of values is [2*All.TimeMax, 4*All.TimeMax]. Note (*): field only exists for TNG100, removed in TNG300 and TNG50.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> ParentID
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span> (**)
          </td><td> <span class="yes"></span>
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> The unique ID (uint64) of the parent of this tracer. Could be a gas cell, star, wind phase cell, or BH. Note (**): TNG100 tracers saved only in the (20) full snapshots! They are saved in all 100 snapshots for TNG300 and TNG50.
          </td></tr>

          </td></tr>

      </div>

      <p>The general unit system is ${\rm kpc}/h$ for lengths, $10^{10} {\rm M}_\odot/h$ for masses, ${\rm km/s}$ for 
      velocities. The frequently occurring $(10^{10} {\rm M}_\odot/h) / (0.978 {\rm Gyr}/h)$ represents mass-over-time 
      in this unit system, and multiplying by 10.22 converts to ${\rm M}_\odot/{\rm yr}$. Comoving quantities can be 
      converted in the corresponding physical ones by multiplying for the appropriate power of the scale factor $a$.
      For instance, to convert a length in physical units it is sufficient to multiply it by $a$, volumes need a 
      factor $a^3$, densities $a^{-3}$ and so on. Note that at redshift $z=0$ the scale factor is $a=1$, so that the 
      numerical values of comoving quantities are the same as their physical counterparts.</p>
      
      <h4>Tagged Metals</h4>
 
      <p>(*) The units of all the entries of <code>GFM_MetalsTagged</code>, except for NSNS, are the same as GFM_Metals: dimensionless mass ratios. If you sum up all the elements of GFM_Metals heavier than Helium, you recover the sum of the three tags SNIa+SNII+AGB. Likewise, the Fe entry of GFM_Metals roughly equals the sum of FeSNIa+FeSNII, modulo the small amount of iron consumed (i.e. negative contribution) by AGB winds. The particular fields are:</p>

      <ul>
        <li> SNIa (0): The total metals ejected by Type Ia supernovae. </li>
        <li> SNII (1): The total metals ejected by Type II supernovae. </li>
        <li> AGB (2): the total metals ejected by stellar winds, which is dominated by AGB stars. </li>
        <li> NSNS (3): the total mass ejected from NS-NS merger events, which are modeled stochastically (i.e. no fractional events) with a DTD scheme similar to that used for SNIa, except with a different $\tau$ value. Note that the units of NSNS are arbitrary. To obtain physical values in units of solar masses, this field must be multiplied by (MyPreferred_NSNS_MassPerEvent/NSNS_MassPerEvent), where MyPreferred_NSNS_MassPerEvent is the amount of mass ejected per NS-NS merger preferred for analysis (e.g. Shen+ 2015 uses here a value of $0.05 M_{\odot}$), and NSNS_MassPerEvent is the value we have set for the simulation, which varies by run. In particular, it is 0.05 for TNG100* and 5000.0 for TNG300* and TNG50*. </li>
        <li> FeSNIa (4): The total iron ejected by Type Ia supernovae alone. </li>
        <li> FeSNII (5): The total iron ejected by Type II supernovae alone. </li>
      </ul>

      <p>(^) Although the two shock finder fields exist in subboxes, they are not updated for each subbox output, but only occasionally, so in general one should NOT use these two fields in subbox snapshots.</p>
      
      <a name="sec1c" class="anchor"></a>

      <h4>Subboxes</h4>
      
      <p>Separate "subbox" cutouts exist for each baryonic run. These are spatial cutouts of fixed comoving 
      size and fixed comoving coordinates, and the primary benefit is that their time resolution is 
      significantly better than that of the main snapshots. This may be useful for some types of analysis or particular 
      science questions, or for making movies. <span class="newtng">There are two subboxes for TNG100 (corresponding to 
      the original Illustris subboxes #0 and #2, the latter increased in size), and three subboxes for TNG50 and TNG300.</span> 
      Two notes of caution: first, the time spacing of the subboxes is not 
      uniform in scale factor or redshift, but scales with the time integration hierarchy of the simulation, and is 
      thus variable, with some discrete factor of two jumps at several points during the simulations. Second, the 
      subboxes, unlike the full box, are not periodic.</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTableSm">

          </tr>

      <a name="sec2" class="anchor"></a>
      <hr class="docs">
      
      <h3>2. Group Catalogs</h3>
      
      <p>There is one group catalog associated with each snapshot, which includes both FoF and Subfind objects. The 
      group files are split into a small number of sub-files, just as with the raw snapshots. 
      <span class="newtng">In TNG, these files are called "fof_subhalo_tab_*", whereas in original Illustris they were 
      called "groups_*"</span> (they are otherwise essentially identical). Every HDF5 group catalog 
      contains the following groups: Header, Group, Subhalo. The IDs of the member particles of each 
      group/subgroup are not stored in the group catalog files. Rather, particles/cells in the snapshot files are 
      ordered according to group membership.</p>
      
      <p>In order to reduce confusion, we adopt the following terminology when referring to different types of objects:</p>
      
      <ul>
        <li>"Group", "FoF Group", and "FoF Halo" all refer to halos.</li>
        <li>"Subgroup", "Subhalo", and "Subfind Group" all refer to subhalos.</li>
        <li>The first (most massive) subgroup of each halo is the "Primary Subgroup" or "Central Subgroup".</li>
        <li>All other following subgroups within the same halo are "Secondary Subgroups", or "Satellite Subgroups".</li>
      </ul>
            
      <a name="sec2a" class="anchor"></a>

      <h4>FoF Halos</h4>
      
      <p>The Group fields are derived with a standard friends-of-friends (FoF) algorithm with linking length $b=0.2$. 
      The FoF algorithm is run on the dark matter particles, and the other types (gas, stars, BHs) are attached to the 
      same groups as their nearest DM particle. The fields for the FoF halo catalog are described in the following table
      (all fields are float32 unless otherwise specified):</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTable">        
          <tr>
          <th> Field </th>
          <th> DataType </th>
          <th> Dimensions </th>
          <th style="width:15%;"> Units </th>
          <th> Description </th>
          </tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupBHMass
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Sum of the BH_Mass field of all blackholes (type 5) in this group.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupBHMdot
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $(10^{10} M_\odot/h) / (0.978Gyr/h)$
          </td><td> Sum of the BH_Mdot field of all blackholes (type 5) in this group.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupCM
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N,3
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Center of mass of the group, computed as the sum of the mass weighted relative coordinates of all particles/cells in the group, of all types. Comoving coordinate. (Available only for the Illustris-3 run)
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupFirstSub
          </td><td> int32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Index into the <code>Subhalo</code> table of the first (i.e. central/primary) Subfind subhalo within this FoF group. Note: This value is signed (or should be interpreted as signed)! In this case, a value of -1 indicates that this FoF group has no subhalos.

          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupMass
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Sum of the individual masses of every particle/cell, of all types, in this group.

          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupNsubs
          </td><td> int32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -

          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupSFR
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ M_\odot / yr $
          </td><td> Sum of the individual star formation rates of all gas cells in this group.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="newtng"> GroupStarMetalFractions
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N,10
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Individual abundances: H, He, C, N, O, Ne, Mg, Si, Fe, total (in this order). Each is the dimensionless ratio of the total mass in that species divided by the total stellar mass, for all stars in the group. The tenth entry contains the 'total' of all other (i.e. untracked) metals. 
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupStarMetallicity
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Mass-weighted average metallicity (Mz/Mtot, where Z = any element above He) of all star particles in this FOF group.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupVel
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N,3
          </td><td> $ km/s/a $
          </td><td> Velocity of the group, computed as the sum of the mass weighted velocities of all particles/cells in this group, of all types. The peculiar velocity is obtained by multiplying this value by $1/a.$
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> GroupWindMass
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Sum of the individual masses of all wind phase gas cells (type 4, BirthTime &lt;= 0) in this group.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_M_Crit200
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Total Mass of this group enclosed in a sphere whose mean density is 200 times the critical density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered.  
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_M_Crit500
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Total Mass of this group enclosed in a sphere whose mean density is 500 times the critical density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered. 
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_M_Mean200
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Total Mass of this group enclosed in a sphere whose mean density is 200 times the mean density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered. 
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_M_TopHat200
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot / h $
          </td><td> Total Mass of this group enclosed in a sphere whose mean density is $\Delta_c$ times the critical density' of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered. $\Delta_c$ derives from the solution of the collapse of a spherical top-hat perturbation (fitting formula from <a href="http://adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/1998ApJ...495...80B">Bryan+ 1998</a>). The subscript 200 can be ignored.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_R_Crit200
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Comoving Radius of a sphere centered at the GroupPos of this Group whose mean density is 200 times the critical density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_R_Crit500
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Comoving Radius of a sphere centered at the GroupPos of this Group whose mean density is 500 times the critical density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_R_Mean200

          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Comoving Radius of a sphere centered at the GroupPos of this Group whose mean density is 200 times the mean density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> Group_R_TopHat200
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ ckpc/h $
          </td><td> Comoving Radius of a sphere centered at the GroupPos of this Group whose mean density is $\Delta_c$ times the critical density of the Universe, at the time the halo is considered.
          </td></tr>
        </table>
      </div>

      <h4>Subfind Subhalos (Galaxies)</h4>
      
      <p>The Subhalo fields are derived with the Subfind algorithm, with modifications to add additional baryonic 
      properties to each subhalo entry. Descriptions of all fields in this subhalo catalog are given in the following 
      table. Note that for all mass calculations by type, wind phase cells are counted as gas.</p>
      
      <div style="text-align:center;">
        <table class="table table-bordered table-condensed eoTable">        
          <tr>
          <th> Field </th>
          <th> DataType </th>
          <th> Dimensions </th>
          <th style="width:15%;"> Units </th>
          <th> Description </th>
          </tr>
          <tr><td class="newtng"> SubhaloFlag
          </td><td> int16
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> -
          </td><td> Flag field indicating suitability of this subhalo for certain types of analysis. If zero, this subhalo should generally be excluded, and is not thought to be of cosmological origin. That is, it may have formed within an existing halo, or is possibly a baryonic fragment of a disk or other galactic structure identified by Subfind. If one, this subhalo should be considered a 'galaxy' or 'satellite' of cosmological origin. (Note: always true for centrals). This field is only present for baryonic runs, and is absent for dark matter only runs. See the <a href="/data/docs/background/#subhaloflag">data release background</a> for details.
          </td></tr>
          <tr><td class="sametng"> SubhaloBHMass
          </td><td> float32
          </td><td> N
          </td><td> $ 10^{10} M_\odot/h $
          </td><td> Sum of the masses of all blackholes in this subhalo.
